## E08-S01 — Export complete workspace data

Status: Done | Release: R1 | Epic: E08 | Dependencies: E07-S05 (Done; post-exit audit repair merged)

Outcome: A workspace owner obtains a private, machine-readable workspace export from Settings → Privacy & Security. This retains the original E08-S01 ID; deletion and retention were split to S01b/S01c for independent review.
Contracts: Product R1 privacy/operations row and §81.2/§81.10; architecture §§457–460, CSV spreadsheet-cell safety and E02 tenancy/session contracts. The R1 identity override replaces old Auth0 references with Keycloak.
Scope: `apps/web/src/auth.ts`, `session-store.ts`, `tenancy.ts`, `ui/routes.ts` and a focused export worker/module; reuse `jobs.ts`, `s3.ts`, current domain reads and object permissions. Add only export job/metadata schema if existing jobs cannot represent cutoff, expiry and one-use download. The export contains UTF-8 CSV finance data and versioned JSON for accounts, source observations, goals, artifacts/source/state, saved analyses, requesting-user conversations and activity attributable to that user, with manifest, currency/date conventions and per-section counts. Other members' private conversations/activity and security secrets are excluded even when the requester owns the workspace. Step-up must prove strong-factor Keycloak auth within 5 minutes through verified `auth_time`/`acr` claims, never a user-supplied timestamp; missing claims fail closed. No self-service restore, PDF export or emailed attachment.
Acceptance: (1) Synthetic two-workspace user requests export after step-up; package has exact decimal-string money, manifest and only requested workspace records, and CSV formula-leading cells are safe. A second member in that workspace has a distinct private chat/activity sentinel that is absent from the owner's package. (2) A foreign/revoked member, stale step-up, expired/used download or changed membership sees a uniform denial with no object bytes. (3) Retry/recovery produces at most one current private package for one idempotency key; 24-hour expiry removes the object, while interrupted jobs can resume safely. Empty sections are named; a failed required section aborts the entire package and reports a retryable error.
Failure/data/limits: Stream bounded chunks instead of loading a workspace into memory; define a fixed test fixture of 2 workspaces, 10k transactions and 2 artifacts. Preserve snapshot consistency without a DB transaction spanning object upload. Private storage, unpredictable key, encryption and download audit are required. Rollback disables creation/download and drains jobs before migration rollback; retain generated exports until their stated expiry unless immediate security deletion is needed.
Verification: Add `npm run test:export` (real disposable PG/Redis/MinIO, synthetic exact-money and hostile tenant/expiry/replay/CSV cases); regress `test:tenancy`, `test:auth`, `test:import-e2e`, `test:artifact-ui`, `test:job-recovery`, `test:e07-exit`; run typecheck, web/worker builds, browser keyboard/download journey, diff/secret gates. Review the actual archive contents and authorization at request, generation and download.

## E08-S01b — Delete account/workspace data durably

Status: Done | Release: R1 | Epic: E08 | Dependencies: E08-S01

Outcome: A step-up protected deletion request immediately revokes access and queued effects, then resumably purges the chosen scope while leaving only a minimal protected tombstone. Founder decision (2026-09-24): deleting an identity preserves finance data in workspaces with other members, while removing that user's membership, sessions and personal content.
Contracts: Product R1 privacy/operations row/§81.10; architecture §§461–464, E02 tenancy, E04 jobs, E05 artifacts and R1 Keycloak override.
Scope: Use existing session revocation, durable jobs/outbox, `s3Delete`, tenant FKs and audit machinery. A deletion coordinator with idempotent checkpoints handles DB domain rows, original/import/export/artifact objects, chat/evidence/state, pending work, grants/caches, external processor deletion where applicable and Keycloak identity when appropriate. Explicit workspace deletion purges that workspace for every member. Personal identity deletion removes the user's membership, sessions, private conversations/activity, settings, grants, exports and identity; financial rows and reproducible evidence in a workspace with remaining members stay available to them. Existing artifacts and their shared state are workspace-owned (no user owner in the schema), so they remain with that workspace; a future user-private artifact field must follow the personal-content purge rule. Pseudonymize retained financial provenance so it cannot relink the departed identity. If the departing user is the last owner of a shared workspace, require a named existing-member successor in the step-up confirmation and transfer ownership atomically with access revocation; a sole-member workspace is purged. Tombstones live outside ordinary restorable tenant data. Do not confuse ordinary archive with privacy erasure.
Acceptance: (1) A two-member shared workspace retains exact transaction/FX/goal totals and artifact access for the remaining member after the other member deletes their identity; the departed member's private chat/activity sentinel, membership, sessions, grants and Keycloak identity are absent, and their old session/late jobs cannot write. (2) A last-owner deletion without a successor is refused before any irreversible effect; with a selected existing-member successor, ownership transfer and revocation are atomic. A sole-member workspace or explicit owner-requested workspace deletion purges all scoped finance, objects and derived data. (3) Repeated request/crash resumes to one completion record; foreign actor and another workspace are untouched. Partial object/processor failure remains visible and retryable, never marked complete.
Failure/data/limits: Never run destructive fault tests on shared/production services. Confirm ownership, successor, scope and consequences accessibly before step-up; deletion is irreversible once purge starts. Use bounded checkpoints (at most 1,000 rows/objects per batch) and a 10k-row synthetic fixture; a retry cannot expose partially purged data. Backup/provider residue is disclosed according to actual schedules. Rollback can restore code but cannot reverse purged data.
Verification: Add `npm run test:deletion` with disposable PG/Redis/MinIO/Keycloak stub, shared-member money/state oracle, private-data sentinel, last-owner handoff, sole-member purge, foreign-tenant/queued-worker/crash/retry/object-failure/zero-target checks; regress `test:tenancy`, `test:job-recovery`, `test:artifact-runtime`, `test:e07-exit`; run typecheck/builds and independent security review.

- Implementation: migration `042_deletions` (workspaces.deletion_requested_at revocation marker, deletion_requests with per-table checkpoint JSONB + FORCE RLS, deletion_tombstones UUID/code-only ledger without RLS + UNIQUE(request_id), narrowed E02-S01 immutable-results trigger allowing DELETE only under an active same-workspace deletion request); `apps/web/src/deletion.ts` (step-up-gated accept with owner/successor rules, synchronous coordinator: cancel-before-revocation, per-step transactions, bounded 1,000-row batches, object deletes before row purges, atomic final steps, same-key resume via deletion-context replay, Keycloak recording stand-in + users anonymize at zero memberships); withTenant revocation gate; withDeletionWorkspace privileged context (request-bound, RLS-scoped); API routes + Privacy & Security deletion section

## E08-S01c — Enforce and disclose retention

Status: In progress | Release: R1 | Epic: E08 | Dependencies: E08-S01b

Outcome: A machine-readable retention catalog and bounded cleanup agree with the privacy text and actual deployed storage/processor behavior.

Local/Deploy split (2026-09-24 refinement against the code): the local slice is E08-S01c-L (implemented and reviewed here); backup/audit/processor periods and privacy sign-off are E08-S01c-D and stay gated on the hosting/processor decision. The parent is not Done until both land. No production purge until backup retention and legal basis are approved.
Contracts: Architecture §§455–456/463/465 and E02 raw-upload 30-day marker; product R1 privacy/operations row.
Scope: Retain canonical observations while the workspace is active; default original uploaded bytes to 30 days after validated import, with explicit retain/unresolved-import holds; export packages expire at 24 hours. Set and enforce periods for AI metadata, Redis/PG job records, security audit and backups only after operational/legal review. Reuse existing source-object markers and queue retention. Give Settings a plain account of active-system, backup and provider timelines.
Acceptance: Synthetic clock advance purges eligible raw files/export bundles once, keeps held source bytes and canonical observations, and retries failed S3 deletes; deleted tenant tombstones outlive backup restoration windows. Catalog, settings text and cleanup code name the same durations. No privacy text claims provider guarantees that have not been qualified in S03.
Verification: Add `npm run test:retention` with disposable PG/MinIO and frozen clock; run `test:upload`, `test:export`, `test:deletion`, `test:job-recovery`, typecheck/builds and privacy-text review. No production purge until backup retention and legal basis are approved.
Open gate: Proposed on 2026-09-24 for the controlled beta: seven-day managed PostgreSQL PITR and 30-day rolling, daily, independently encrypted backup copies in Backblaze B2 EU Central; apply seven-day Object Lock to backup generations, expire all object versions by the approved 30-day schedule, and keep external deletion tombstones at least 45 days so they outlive the oldest restorable backup. Backblaze rules: https://www.backblaze.com/docs/cloud-storage-object-lock and https://www.backblaze.com/docs/cloud-storage-lifecycle-rules . These are proposed operational values, not approved privacy text. Production backup, audit and processor retention periods still require the actual hosting/processor inventory and privacy sign-off; keep Draft until those values are recorded.

## E08-S01c-L — Enforce local retention (upload bytes, export bundles, tombstone floor)

Status: Done | Release: R1 | Epic: E08 | Dependencies: E08-S01b (Done at `1c4f770`)

Outcome: Bounded local cleanup enforces the arch 455-456 periods that need no provider: original upload bytes ~30 days after validated import (explicit retain/unresolved holds), export bundles 24 hours (cross-workspace sweep closing the S01 lazy-only gap), and deletion tombstones never purged (45-day floor exported for S02). AI metadata, queue records, security audit and backups are cataloged as deployment-gated with no enforced period and no fake claim.
Contracts: Architecture §§455–456/463/465 and E02 raw-upload 30-day marker; product R1 privacy/operations row; S01 export expiry + S01b tombstones/objects.
Scope: `apps/web/src/retention.ts` (machine-readable RETENTION_CATALOG, per-workspace sweep contexts bound to expiry-index rows, purgeImportBytes, sweepRetention across both expiry indexes); migration `043_retention` (imports.retain_original, export_expiry_index + import_expiry_index ID-only discovery tables without RLS, backfill of existing markers); uploads.ts writes the import index on STAGED and (new) sets the 30-day marker on REJECTED; export.ts writes/clears the export index on accept/consume/expire/fail/purge; privacy page renders the catalog (enforced values + deployment-gated names). No scheduler/cron (S01c-D owns cadence), no member sweep endpoint (tests drive the sweeper; the deployed cron reuses it), no backup/audit/processor enforcement.
Acceptance: (1) Synthetic backdated clocks purge eligible STAGED/REJECTED bytes once (object + source_objects rows gone; canonical observations + import row stay), keep retain=true and unmarked/unresolved bytes, and clean only consumed index rows. (2) Backdated READY exports expire via the cross-workspace sweep (object gone, keys nulled); broken storage stays READY with failed counts and converges after restore. (3) Backdated tombstones survive every sweep; catalog, settings text and cleanup code name the same durations; gated classes carry no period. (4) Tombstone floor constant (45d) is exported for S02.
Failure/data/limits: Sweeps are read-committed per package/import with bounded batches (≤1,000 rows/objects); failed object deletes stay visible with counts and retry. Tombstones are never deleted by any sweep. Deterministic via backdated markers (no wall-clock races).
Verification: `npm run test:retention` (disposable PG `moneo_e08_retention` + MinIO, real bytes/objects); regress `test:upload`, `test:export`, `test:deletion`, `test:job-recovery`, `test:tenancy` (043-first rollback chain); typecheck, build:web, `staging:smoke`, diff/secret gates; independent review.
Review focus: cross-workspace context bypass scope (index-bound, RLS-scoped reads only), RLS on new tables (indexes intentionally ID-only like job_dispatch_index), marker writes on every terminal path, retain/hold correctness, tombstone immunity, catalog-text-code agreement, scheduler-shaped scope creep.
Rollout/rollback: Additive migration + new module; rollback drops the indexes/column (synthetic data only). Disable by not running the sweeper; no cron exists yet.

## E08-S02 — Restore safely and prove operational recovery

Status: In progress | Release: R1 | Epic: E08 | Dependencies: E08-S01b, E08-S01c

Outcome: An operator can restore into an isolated restricted environment, replay external deletion tombstones, verify exact financial evidence and reopen traffic only after checks pass.

Local/Deploy split (2026-09-24 refinement against the code): the local slice is E08-S02-L (isolated pg_dump/pg_restore + tombstone replay + hash verification on disposable services); provider-native PITR, managed-Valkey/Spaces behavior and measured RPO/RTO on the selected host are E08-S02-D and stay open. The parent is not Done until both land.

Outcome: An operator can restore into an isolated restricted environment, replay external deletion tombstones, verify exact financial evidence and reopen traffic only after checks pass.
Contracts: Architecture §§393/463–464, R1 Docker/Keycloak override and E01/E02/E04 durability contracts. Initial internal targets: provider-native RPO ≤15 minutes where PITR exists; independent logical-backup RPO ≤24 hours; RTO ≤4 hours. These are engineering targets, not customer SLAs.
Scope: Pin production PostgreSQL, persistent Docker-hosted Keycloak, queue and object-storage configuration, TLS/network boundary, encrypted backup destination and recovery credentials for the selected host; add minimal operator backup/restore scripts and a redacted runbook. Reconcile PG jobs/outbox/Redis after restore and test migration/deploy rollback. No ordinary staging copy of real customer data and no user-facing restore UI.
Acceptance: Restore synthetic snapshot with exact pre-failure transaction/FX/goal/artifact hashes; apply newer protected tombstones before serving and prove deleted workspace stays inaccessible. Measure backup age and elapsed restore against targets; corrupted/missing backup fails closed. Document owner, alert, restore point, traffic gate, rollback and Keycloak recovery.
Verification: Add `npm run test:restore` for disposable PG/MinIO/Redis/Keycloak, a fresh-host drill and `test:job-recovery`, `test:e07-exit`, `staging:smoke`, typecheck/builds. Record measured values and artifacts, not a claimed pass from script existence.
Open gate: Hosting recommendation researched 2026-09-24: DigitalOcean FRA1 Basic 8 GiB Droplet for pinned Docker web/worker and production Keycloak, managed PostgreSQL Standard 4 GiB with separate application/Keycloak databases and users, managed Valkey 1 GiB (subject to BullMQ compatibility/durability drill), and private FRA1 Spaces for active objects; use a separate Backblaze B2 EU Central account/bucket for daily client-encrypted PostgreSQL dumps and active-object copies, with a protected deletion-tombstone copy. Estimated provider base is US$128.90/month (Droplet $48 + PostgreSQL $60.90 + Valkey $15 + Spaces $5), plus B2 usage, taxes, domain, and any extra capacity. DigitalOcean PostgreSQL offers seven-day PITR; restore creates a new cluster. B2 EU Central stores in Amsterdam and has independent credentials. Sources: https://www.digitalocean.com/pricing/droplets ; https://www.digitalocean.com/pricing/managed-databases ; https://docs.digitalocean.com/products/databases/postgresql/details/features/ ; https://docs.digitalocean.com/products/databases/postgresql/details/limits/ ; https://docs.digitalocean.com/products/spaces/details/pricing/ ; https://www.backblaze.com/docs/cloud-storage-data-regions ; https://www.backblaze.com/cloud-storage/pricing . This is a recommendation, not provisioned or qualified infrastructure. Select the host/region/domain, confirm the budget and privacy terms, prove Spaces adapter/encryption and managed Valkey behavior, then measure the isolated restore before Ready or customer data.

## E08-S02-L — Prove isolated restore with tombstone replay (local)

Status: Done | Release: R1 | Epic: E08 | Dependencies: E08-S01c-L (Done at `36739e4`)

Outcome: A pg_dump/pg_restore drill into an isolated disposable database plus MinIO object snapshot proves exact financial evidence survives restore and newer deletion tombstones re-purge resurrected data before any traffic reopens.
Contracts: Architecture §§393/463–464 (restore into isolation, tombstone replay, no casual restore into use), R1 Docker/Keycloak override; S01b tombstones, S01c 45-day floor, E02 durability contracts. Internal targets (RPO ≤15 min provider-native where PITR exists, ≤24 h logical, RTO ≤4 h) are engineering targets, not SLAs; local runs record measured values only.
Scope: `apps/web/src/restore.ts` (canonical evidence hashing, pg_dump/restore orchestration via local PG18 binaries, tombstone replay for workspace + identity scopes incl. MinIO prefix purge, verification against pre-failure hashes, migration re-run + app boot check) plus a redacted operator runbook (`docs/implementation/RESTORE-RUNBOOK.md`: owner, alert, restore point, traffic gate, rollback, Keycloak recovery). Tests drive the module with real pg_dump/pg_restore/MinIO against disposable databases; traffic never touches the restored DB except the drill's own boot check. No PITR/vendor drill, no managed-Valkey/Spaces proof, no production credentials (S02-D).
Acceptance: (1) Restored KEEP workspace hashes equal pre-failure values exactly (transactions/FX-manual/goals/artifacts/analyses); deleted DEL workspace rows are present in the raw restore (resurrection proven) then gone after replaying tombstones newer than the restore point, with DEL objects purged and KEEP objects intact. (2) Corrupted/missing backup fails closed (nonzero, target untouched). (3) migrate() re-runs cleanly on the restored tree and the app boots + serves tenant reads. (4) Runbook names owner/alert/restore-point/traffic-gate/rollback/Keycloak-recovery; Keycloak live drill stays a deployed gate.
Failure/data/limits: Synthetic data only; disaster simulated by dropping the live drill DB. Restores go to fresh isolated databases, never the live one. Bounded fixture (exact-money rows + one object per workspace); durations recorded, not promised.
Verification: `npm run test:restore` (disposable PG `moneo_e08_restore*` + MinIO, real binaries); regress `test:job-recovery`, `test:deletion` (tombstone source), `test:e07-exit`; typecheck, build:web, `staging:smoke`, diff/secret gates; independent review.
Review focus: restore-target isolation (never live), tombstone-window correctness (only newer-than-restore-point replay), hash canonicalization (decimal strings, ordering, no float), superuser-scope discipline in operator code, traffic-gate honesty, backup-secret hygiene.
Rollout/rollback: Operator tooling only; no app routes. Rollback = remove scripts/module use; drill databases are disposable.

## E08-S02-D — Qualify provider recovery (RPO/RTO on the selected host)

Status: Draft | Release: R1 | Epic: E08 | Dependencies: E08-S02-L, hosting selection

Outcome: The selected host's PITR + independent logical backups meet the internal RPO/RTO targets in a measured isolated drill, with encrypted destinations, tested Keycloak recovery and redacted evidence.
Contracts: Architecture §393/§§12227–12372 recovery targets; S02 open gate (DigitalOcean/Backblaze proposal or the zero-cost candidate).
Scope: Provisioned provider drill only after hosting selection: managed PG PITR restore to a new cluster, Valkey/Spaces behavior, B2 encrypted copies + Object Lock, Keycloak recovery drill, measured backup age vs targets. No customer data before it passes.
Acceptance: Measured backup age ≤ RPO targets and elapsed restore ≤ RTO in the isolated drill; corrupted-backup fail-closed; tombstones outlive the oldest restorable backup; runbook updated with actual owner/alert/credentials-handling.
Verification: Provider drill evidence + reviewer sign-off. Not runnable locally — stays open until hosting is chosen.

Zero-cost candidate researched 2026-09-24 after founder asked for a free setup: Oracle Cloud Always Free A1 in Frankfurt (currently 2 Arm OCPUs/12 GiB RAM, 200 GiB block, 20 GiB object storage after trial) for Docker web/worker/Keycloak and Redis with persistent AOF; Supabase Free PostgreSQL (500 MiB) for app and Keycloak in separate schemas/roles; OCI private Object Storage for active objects; separate Backblaze B2 EU Central free 10 GiB for encrypted daily database/object backups and deletion tombstones. This is a test candidate, not a qualified production choice: Oracle free shape capacity and idle reclamation, Supabase inactivity pausing/no native backups, 500 MiB DB and 10 GiB independent backup ceilings, Arm image compatibility, self-hosted Redis durability, object S3 compatibility, TLS/domain and the four-hour restore target must be proved. Self-hosting Redis needs an explicit exception to the architecture's managed-stateful-service rule. Supabase free has no PITR, so the provider-native ≤15-minute RPO target is unavailable; the independent ≤24-hour target remains. Never use paid trial capacity as proof of a lasting free deployment. Sources: https://docs.oracle.com/en-us/iaas/Content/FreeTier/freetier_topic-Always_Free_Resources.htm ; https://docs.oracle.com/en-us/iaas/Content/General/Concepts/regions.htm ; https://supabase.com/pricing ; https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/backups ; https://supabase.com/docs/guides/platform/free-project-pausing ; https://www.backblaze.com/cloud-storage/pricing . Keep S02 Draft until an account can provision the Always Free shape and the full recovery/security/load drill passes on that exact setup.

## E08-S03 — Qualify production AI and customer-data disclosure

Status: In progress | Release: R1 | Epic: E08 | Dependencies: E08-S01c, E04-S07

Outcome: Every production customer-data model call uses an evaluated, available no-training/ZDR route with honest processor/region/retention disclosure; absence of a compliant route returns recoverable unavailability.

Local/Deploy split (2026-09-24 refinement against ai-dispatch.ts): the fail-closed production-route policy, capability checks at reserve/execute/fallback, processor inventory and synthetic evaluations are E08-S03-L (no live calls, no customer data); the credentialed synthetic live qualification on the selected no-training/ZDR route is E08-S03-D and stays open. The parent is not Done until both land. No live no-training/ZDR route is qualified without evidence.
Contracts: Product deployment constraints; architecture §130 (production `data_collection: "deny"`, `zdr: true`, parameter support on every route/fallback), §§465 and E04-S07 rubric. Development free/training-permitted routes stay separate.
Scope: Reuse `ai-dispatch.ts`, `ai-eval.ts`, policy/usage ledger and bounded synthetic rubric. Pin production models/routes and credentials separately from development; enforce fail-closed capability checks at each dispatch and fallback. Inventory OpenRouter, underlying models and subprocessors, actual processing regions, retention and contracts. Do not send real-customer data during qualification.
Acceptance: Unsupported ZDR/no-collection setting, unavailable compliant provider, missing credentials and an attempted free-route fallback all fail closed without request leakage; permitted calls respect budgets, cancellation and redacted telemetry. Versioned synthetic eval meets the predeclared E04 rubric for grounded claims, tools, mapping and edits; disclosures match signed processor terms and observed config.
Verification: `npm run test:ai-dispatch`, `test:ai-eval`, `test:chat`, `test:deep-analysis`, `test:artifact-ai`; add a bounded production-route policy/transport test, then a separate credentialed synthetic live qualification with exact model/version/cost and no customer records. Typecheck/builds and independent privacy/security review.
Open gate: Qualified route availability, processor agreements and actual disclosure text depend on external providers and privacy review; record evidence before Ready for live customer qualification.

## E08-S03-L — Fail-closed production-route policy with honest disclosure (local, synthetic)

Status: Done | Release: R1 | Epic: E08 | Dependencies: E08-S01c-L (Done at `36739e4`; E04-S07 Done pre-E07)

Outcome: Production-route dispatches require an explicit pinned no-training/ZDR capability record at reserve, rechecked at execute/fallback; anything else (unqualified flag, unsupported ZDR/data-collection setting, missing credentials, free-model fallback attempt) fails closed without request leakage. The processor inventory names development reality and pending production qualification without inventing terms; versioned synthetic evaluations run offline.
Contracts: Product deployment constraints; architecture §130 (`data_collection: "deny"`, `zdr: true`, parameter support on every route/fallback), §§465 and E04-S07 rubric. Development free/training-permitted routes stay separate and never serve production.
Scope: `loadProductionRouteConfig()` capability gate (flag + deny/ZDR settings + pinned non-free model + credential presence, names only) enforced in reserveDispatch and rechecked in executeReserved pre-attempt and pre-retry (release with route_forbidden); transport refuses free-variant models on the production route without sending; `apps/web/src/ai-processors.ts` machine-readable inventory + Settings disclosure (dev actual, prod pending); versioned synthetic eval evidence offline; no live calls, no customer records, no new tables.
Acceptance: (1) Each misconfiguration denies with route_forbidden and a recording transport proves zero requests left the process; budgets/permits unconsumed beyond the released hold. (2) Dequalification between reserve and execute releases without provider I/O. (3) Free-model production attempts fail closed at reserve and at the transport. (4) Disclosure text matches observed config (dev) and names every pending production term without asserting it. (5) Frozen E04-rubric synthetic cases pass offline with versions recorded.
Failure/data/limits: Synthetic fixtures only; production-credentials env names read, values never logged. Reservation holds release on every deny path (no stranded PENDING from policy denies).
Verification: New `npm run test:prod-policy` (disposable PG, recording transports, env-matrix); regress `test:ai-dispatch`, `test:ai-eval`, `test:chat`, `test:deep-analysis`, `test:artifact-ai`, typecheck/builds; independent review.
Review focus: TOCTOU between reserve/execute (recheck completeness), fallback paths that could downgrade to development, free-model detection gaps, credential/secret handling in config/errors, disclosure over-claims, eval oracle independence.
Rollout/rollback: Code-only capability gate defaulting to deny; rollback = unset qualification flags (production stays forbidden). No migration.

## E08-S05 — Establish load, failure and cost release gates

Status: In progress | Release: R1 | Epic: E08 | Dependencies: E08-S04

Outcome: The deployed candidate has measured safe capacity and recovery limits for an invited beta, with alerting that exposes failures without financial payloads.

Local/Deploy split (2026-09-24 refinement): repeatable local measurement on a fixed synthetic dataset with failure drills is E08-S05-L; numeric p95/memory/queue/cost budgets for the chosen host and beta cohort are E08-S05-D. Local numbers are reference measurements, never a pass for a different deployment.

Outcome: The deployed candidate has measured safe capacity and recovery limits for an invited beta, with alerting that exposes failures without financial payloads.
Contracts: Architecture job/dispatch limits, R1 operations targets; existing E02 100k-row import cap, E03 10k-row query target, E06 projection ≤2s and E07 Home ≤2s local targets. These are starting budgets for measurement, not external SLAs.
Scope: Use a fixed synthetic 2-workspace/100-account/10k-transaction dataset plus one 100k-row boundary import; measure 1/5/20 concurrent users through import, Home/query, projection, 12 pinned artifacts and AI reservations. Record p50/p95, memory, queue depth, cost and rejection reason. Drill worker death, Redis loss, provider outage, cancellation and restore using disposable services. Add only missing redacted counters/alerts and backpressure controls shown necessary by the measurements.
Acceptance: Exact-money and tenant assertions remain true at every load; caps reject safely rather than overspend or silently drop work. Provider and queue failures remain visible/recoverable; cost reservations bound concurrent usage. Record maximum supported beta concurrency and any budget miss with a concrete fix or reduced cohort limit. Full R1 journey passes on the latest candidate.
Verification: Add `npm run test:e08-load` with repeatable dataset/config and machine-readable summary; run `test:job-recovery`, `test:ai-dispatch`, `test:e07-exit`, `staging:smoke`, failure gate, builds and privacy scan. Rerun affected cases after each fix; no destructive drills on shared/production services.
Open gate: Before Ready, predeclare numeric p95, memory, queue-wait, safe-rejection and cost budgets for the chosen host and beta cohort. Existing local Home/projection targets are reference measurements, not a pass for a different deployment.

## E08-S05-L — Measure repeatable local load and failure behavior (synthetic)

Status: Done | Release: R1 | Epic: E08 | Dependencies: E08-S04-L (Done; local candidate fixed at its merge)

Outcome: A repeatable local harness on a fixed synthetic dataset (2 workspaces, 100 accounts, 10k transactions, 12 pinned artifacts, one 100k-row boundary import) measures 1/5/20 concurrent users through import, Home/query, projection, artifacts and AI reservations, plus worker-death/Redis-loss/provider-outage/cancel drills, with exact-money and tenant assertions true at every load and caps rejecting safely.
Contracts: Architecture job/dispatch limits; E02 100k-row import cap, E03 10k-row query target, E06 projection ≤2s and E07 Home ≤2s local targets (reference measurements, not external SLAs).
Scope: `test/e08-load.test.ts` (+ `test:e08-load` script) driving real HTTP/PG/Redis/MinIO/ClamAV/worker paths; machine-readable summary to the approved temp dir; redacted console lines (counts/latencies only). No new alerting backend or backpressure controls unless measurements demand them (record the decision). No numeric host-budget promises.
Acceptance: (1) All reads exact at every concurrency (totals equal the independent oracle; tenant swaps deny). (2) 100k-row import completes within parser ceilings with exact multiplicity/provenance. (3) Redis loss + cancel + provider outage during load converge visibly (recovery completes, no duplicate effects, cost bounded, failures typed). (4) Over-budget AI reservations deny with HTTP 429 safe rejections without overspend. (5) Summary records p50/p95, durations, queue/cost figures and environment for the ledger. Provenance is sampled (source linkage), not just counted; B totals and projection determinism are asserted, not assumed.
Failure/data/limits: Synthetic fixed dataset (seeded); drills use the suite's own disposable Redis DB (flush) and synthetic jobs only. Durations are measurements. CI includes the suite only if it fits the job budget (record decision).
Verification: `npm run test:e08-load` green twice (repeatability); regress `test:job-recovery`, `test:ai-dispatch`, `test:e07-exit`; typecheck/builds; independent review.
Review focus: Dataset determinism, oracle independence (not recomputed by implementation code), rejection safety (no silent drops/overspend), redacted telemetry, CI-time suitability, invented-SLA language.
Rollout/rollback: Test-only plus any measured-necessary controls; no migration expected. Rollback = remove the suite/controls.

- Assignee / branch: Orchestrator/implementer this session / `story/e08-s05-load` (deleted after merge)
- Base SHA / heads: base `f361f8f` (= local main at branch-off); impl `9ef46e9`; fix/reviewed `5e42bf7` (candidate; no post-review code changes)
- Implementation: `test/e08-load.test.ts` (+ `test:e08-load` script) only — no product code, no alerting backend, no backpressure controls, no host budgets. Fixed dataset (2 workspaces, 100 accounts, 10k transactions via generate_series oracle, 12 pinned artifacts, 20 readers, one 99,999-row boundary import at the header-inclusive parser ceiling + one over-ceiling rejection probe); machine-readable summary to the OS temp dir; redacted console (counts/latencies only)
- Measured local values, Windows 11, i5-12450HX, Node v22.23.2, local PG18/Redis/MinIO/ClamAV (not host promises; two full green runs 608 s + 599 s with consistent figures): readers1 p50/p95 237–267 ms; readers5 p50 ~438–474/p95 ~470–491 ms; readers20 p50 ~1.5–1.9 s/p95 ~1.6–1.9 s; projection A ~1.1–1.6 s / B ~0.8–1.0 s (E06 ≤2 s reference met); parse100k ~23–28 s; commit100k ~567–569 s (~175–230 rows/s per-row chunk path); import total ~590–598 s; RSS 118–120 → 169–174 MB (bounded); cost done 5/held 0 within budget 12; Redis-loss rebuilt + applied once; cancel zero-effect; outage PENDING typed
- Tests: `npm run test:e08-load` 0 (5/5: exact A+B totals at 1/5/20 + swap denial, projection determinism run-twice, boundary count + provenance samples + row-limit rejection, Redis-loss/cancel/outage convergence with effect counts, 429 mapping + cost invariant)

## E08-S06 — Validate a controlled cohort and decide the next release

Status: Draft | Release: R1 | Epic: E08 | Dependencies: E08-S05 and all E08 lifecycle/privacy gates

Outcome: Invited, consenting external users try the complete R1 loop; the founder records an explicit go/iterate/no-go decision from predeclared evidence. Public release remains a separate founder decision.
Contracts: Product R1 validation commitment/§76 and EPICS.md E08 exit. No customer financial data before S01/S01b/S01c–S05 pass and required processor/privacy arrangements exist.
Scope: Before recruitment, founder fixes cohort size, invitation criteria, observation window, stopping rules, wrong-claim severity and decision thresholds. Collect minimal anonymized aggregates for import repair, unsupported claims, artifact edit/repair effort, time to first useful Home/tool, AI spend and repeat/reopened-tool use; provide an opt-out/deletion path. Avoid a custom analytics platform and use existing redacted events plus a small survey/observation sheet.
Acceptance: Cohort completes the agreed window or a predeclared stop rule triggers; report denominators, missing observations, adverse cases, actual cost and explicit decision. R1 review accounts for every baseline capability. Write R2 stories only from observed findings, not the synthetic E07 demonstration.
Verification: Consent/privacy check, deployed end-to-end journey, deletion/export request drill, aggregate reconciliation and founder-signed decision record. Never claim positive demand from E07's synthetic test.
Open gate: Founder confirmed on 2026-09-24 that sample, observation window and go/iterate/no-go rubric will be set immediately before E08-S06. Record them before recruitment or results review; privacy/legal/operational sign-off and public-release choice remain separate.

Preparation record (2026-09-24, local phase — no cohort, no recruitment, no users contacted, no customer data):
- Ready now: complete R1 loop demonstrable locally (E07 exit); export/delete drills exercisable via Settings → Privacy & Security; redacted event vocabulary available for aggregates (request/command IDs, counts, latencies, cost minor units — never finance payloads); opt-out/deletion path implemented (S01b); decision-record shape below.
- Still required from the founder immediately before S06 (unchanged open gate): cohort size, invitation criteria, observation window, stopping rules, wrong-claim severity levels, go/iterate/no-go thresholds, survey/observation sheet, privacy/legal/operational sign-off.
- Decision record template (to be signed at S06 close): denominators (invited/active/completed), missing observations with reasons, adverse cases (wrong claims with severity, data-loss/privacy incidents), actual AI spend vs budget, time-to-first-Home/tool distributions, repeat/reopened-tool use, export/delete drill results, aggregate reconciliation notes, explicit go/iterate/no-go with rationale. R2 stories may only be written from observed findings.
- Blocked until: all E08 lifecycle/privacy gates pass (S01c-D, S02-D, S03-D, S04-D, S05-D) + hosting/processor selection + the founder decisions above. E08 as a release is NOT Done.
